### 2026-10-09 — PS5 firmware 13.60 field failure from all-on #232: deterministic 16 MiB sparse precommit overflow

- User tested *installable folder* created from [all-on #232](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/37926886141), artifacts from source SHA `e5d39849`, on real firmware `0x13600007`. Evidence files: `boot-trace(4).txt`, `heap(7).log`, `stderr(8).log`. Elevation, writable persistent root, Vulkan launcher, Nlib and game selection worked. **Two successive game-launch attempts failed** with `EDEN_JIT_SPARSE_COMMIT_FAILED used=2113536 next=16777216 capacity=16777216`, `Game load failed: std::bad_alloc`; game UI was reopened afterward. The JIT sparse probe and RX/RW reservations SUCCEEDED. Critical interpretation: the 16 MiB failure is the **static over-capacity precommit request**, BEFORE PS5 direct physical mapping was attempted (no associated EDEN_JIT_SPARSE_OOM/MAP_FAILED). Earlier speculation that 12 GiB physical RAM was exhausted was wrong. Four A64 worker arenas were reserved (three large, final core 16 MiB); the final 16 MiB core necessarily fails when Dynarmic's 16 MiB PRELUDE_COMMIT_SIZE hint is added to ~2 MiB already written by its constant pool. Host CI mocks missed this edge despite all-on #232 going green.
- Fixed all-title sparse JIT precommit via `headless/jit-sparse-commit.h` and derived Dynarmic `BlockOfCode::EnsureMemoryCommitted` in `headless/CMakeLists.txt`: request up to the available **virtual** bytes without overflow; do not alter Xbyak's actual code-emission bounds, do not silently fall back to dense, and never treat virtual bytes as physically committed until native mapping succeeds. Emits `EDEN_JIT_SPARSE_PRECOMMIT_CLAMPED` only on a clipped hint. No FC27-specific branch, no experimental profile toggle, no approved UI changes, no protected shipping-branch change.
- Strengthened executable `tools/check-jit-sparse-host.py` to replay exact `2113536 + 16777216 > 16777216` values with a real host-mocked `Common::ReserveSparseJitCode` 16 MiB arena, commit to full boundary, byte alias and full cleanup; also checks invalid written positions, no unsigned overflow, and ordinary non-clamped growth. Updated architecture source gate.
- Separately reported symptom **FC27 not present in Library** cannot be proven from these three logs; no ROM filename inventory, directory listing or FC27 scan failure is captured. Existing `EdenServices::games()` did silently skip non-regular/stat-failed NSP/XCI and could publish an empty list on a native directory I/O error. Added single worker-thread `EDEN_ROM_SCAN` inventory diagnostics and first three failing stat filenames; on an enumeration error throw `std::system_error` so `Launcher::finish_scan` retains its prior list instead of emptying the UI. No files deleted or migrated. `tools/check-home-media.py` asserts these contracts.
- **PENDING** native build/CI for corrected source, and actual PS5 game-boot proof. No claim FC27 was restored or Zelda boots yet. Next run is **one** dev `[full-build] [test-all-on]` compilation/package validation. Once tested on hardware, collect `EDEN_JIT_SPARSE_PRECOMMIT_CLAMPED`, first gameplay progress and `EDEN_ROM_SCAN` receipts; if FC27 still absent, verify its physical NSP/XCI path under selected root without deleting `/data/prosperoeden`.

### 2026-10-09 — After R236 FC27 13-minute audit: first executable UI + bounded-cache contention changes (NOT hardware-qualified)

- Continued actual repo code work instead of treating the earlier diagnostic as a fix. Branch `dev/ps5-sparse-jit`; no direct Mac or PS5 writes, no shipping/approved launcher-art modifications.
- **Launcher GL stream root candidate:** actual `headless/prosperoeden/pe/gfx/gl_batch.cpp` called `glBufferData(GL_STREAM_DRAW)` on the SAME VBO **every draw frame** even if there were no changes to required capacity. Real PS5 launcher log showed 130 ms draw and two 33 ms texture uploads, but cause attribution to this VBO is a hypothesis until console A/B. Implemented 3 independent GL VBOs and associated VAOs with round-robin reuse, capacity growth only on demand (power-of-two, minimum 512 `Instance`), no per-frame same-size `glBufferData`. Retains `glBufferSubData` updates and the same draw calls, shaders, art and layout. GL driver synchronization still serializes reuse if GPU is >3 frames behind; correctness preserved. `GlBatch::release` deletes all 3 correctly. New `stream_ring_policy.hpp` and host C++ regression `tools/check-ps5-ui-stream-buffer.py` covering 18 frame sizes: 6 real allocations vs 18 previously, all 3 buffers reuse, overflow check. Tests wired into all-on workflow.

For PS5 **dev profile only**, at the beginning of every game session, apply 8 bounded PAUSE/try_lock retries before normal blocking mutex; every failed retry still invokes `mutex.lock()`. Release build stays 0; no removal/bypass of locks or output correctness. Dev-settings manual override (if supplied) now clamped to ≤32 to avoid starvation. New source+model regression `tools/check-ps5-dev-cache-contention.py` wired into workflow. **No proven FC27 FPS gain or soft-hang qualification yet**. Compare `EDEN_DEV_GUEST cache_lock_contended` vs `cache_lock_blocked` and frame tails on next PS5 test.

Track per-thread cached bytes with owner-write relaxed atomics (no shared counter on allocation hotpath) and expose approximate `EDEN_PERF_HEAP tcache_bytes` in existing snapshot. TLS destructor drains/free-lists and zeros held bytes. Host 8-thread heap harness includes multi-bin pressure, post-free and post-worker-join upper bounds; test script edited **not executed**.

### 2026-10-09 — Development GPU timestamp query-pool explicit destruction [skip ci]

- Corrected an API lifecycle overstatement in previous checkpoint: Vulkan VkQueryPool is a device child that must be explicitly destroyed before its VkDevice. Generator now substitutes Scheduler::~Scheduler() only for this dev probe cleanup: if a timestamp pool exists, requests worker stop, wakes it, joins the jthread, waits on device idle and invokes vkDestroyQueryPool while Device remains live, then clears process-global probe. Ordinary probe-OFF path retains standard jthread destructor behavior. Per-title epoch remains to reject stale device state.

### 2026-10-09 — PS5 direct-memory map rollback ownership hardening [skip ci]

### 2026-10-09 — Sparse JIT per-thread positive-page hint replaces needless global lock [skip ci]

- Pinned Dynarmic A64 CompileBlock checks BlockOfCode::EnsureMemoryCommitted(MINIMUM_REMAINING_CODESIZE) once per block, while actual PS5 2MiB RX/RW direct-memory commit happens only at page-growth boundaries. Before this patch, every CompileBlock entered process-global sparse_jit_mutex/map lookup, contending across guest cores even when no physical commit required. Added trivial thread_local CachedCommit{executable,committed,generation}; atomic sparse_jit_generation epoch increments on both reservation and release. Only when pointer/epoch match AND requested<=previously committed does it return lock-free. Genuine page growth still uses original mutex, ownership, memset, W^X mapping, and allocator; success updates TLS positive record. No extra heap allocation in hot path; requires guest workers quiescent before destructor (existing contract). Tools/check-ps5-architecture.py source assertions added, tests/PS5 native validation NOT RUN. Potential positive hotpath improvement, not proven FPS.

Added nonblocking query_in_flight atomic claim after timestamp CAS; if another query is in progress, return previous known data without blocking renderer; owner releases after handling known/unavailable state. Dev profile only collects up to 16 `EDEN_PS5_DMEM_PROBE_SLOW latency_ns=... known=...` when kernel request + update >=2ms using CLOCK_MONOTONIC; shipping no extra clock reads. ### 2026-10-09 — Stop repeat heap piece leaks on growth mspace failure [skip ci]

- Found a real OOM escalation in `headless/heap_arenas.inc`: when a new physical 128 MiB backing commit succeeded but Sony `sceLibcMspaceCreate` failed, the piece became an orphan. Each subsequent allocation that did not fit an existing mspace could commit another whole physical piece and fail again. This could exhaust direct memory without increasing usable heap capacity.
- Source now latches a growth-lock-owned failure state after the first *post-startup* mspace creation failure. Successful earlier mspaces remain usable. No subsequent physical piece is committed for that error in the same process. Failed piece is **not** unmapped or freed because physical ownership and mspace-failure cleanup semantics are not proven, and no normal heap reclaim is claimed. Extends `tools/check-heap-growth.py` with a deterministic `growth-mspace-fail` mock and memory accounting checks.

### 2026-10-09 — Protect sparse page-table remaps from unexpected fixed-map aliases [skip ci]

- `MapSlot()` formerly returned false if `sceKernelMapDirectMemory(..., MAP_FIXED)` returned a **different** virtual address, but did not unmap that successful foreign alias before the caller released or reused its backing physical page. This could leave an orphan physical alias under unusual kernel behavior. Now a successful unexpected alias is explicitly unmapped before the caller receives failure; an error with a mutated address aborts rather than guessing mapping ownership. Ordinary successful fixed maps and regular failures (unchanged address) retain prior behavior. Source preflight contract added; native firmware semantics still unqualified.

### 2026-10-09 — Remove global dev HLE profiler mutex/map from every guest IPC request [skip ci]

The prior `RecordHle` acquired a **single mutex** and performed `std::map` lookup on **every service command** across guest cores, injecting profiler contention and allocator pressure directly into FC27's workload. Its map also retained non-owned service-name pointers across title lifetimes.
- Introduced `headless/hle_counters.h`: 4,096 bounded preallocated service-name/command slots; stable text-content FNV-1a hash instead of pointer identity; owned 80-byte diagnostic name per slot; lock only while first registering a new pair, then atomic call/ns totals with no lock or map allocation for normal requests. Snapshots iterate atomics without contending with guest HLE dispatch. An overflow aggregate preserves total accounting when all slots are occupied. No change to game HLE behavior or IPC sequencing, only diagnostic aggregation.
- Integrated a dedicated C++20 eight-thread host stress/recycled-service-name/fixed-table-overflow fixture `tools/check-hle-counter-contention.py` into the explicit GitHub all-on preflight. ### 2026-10-09 — HLE first-registration race closed before native compile [skip ci]

- Review identified a publication race in the new fixed HLE profiler: if two workers first saw the same empty hash slot, the second waited for the registration mutex; after the first published the same service/command, the second could skip that slot and insert a duplicate farther in the table. Corrected the under-lock recheck to merge into the newly published **same** entry, not skip it. Host eight-thread fixture now explicitly requires all 35 unique service/command identities, each reported once, with unchanged exact aggregate counts and no overflow for the normal stress case. ### 2026-10-09 — Hard bound per-command HLE diagnostic probe depth [skip ci]

- Prevented a diagnostic self-inflicted worst-case: with 4,096 hash slots, a game with thousands of distinct service/command pairs could make **every subsequent hot HLE call scan thousands of slots** before incrementing its overflow counter. Each call now probes at most 64 consecutive slots; collisions/near-full clusters spill into atomic aggregate overflow. The source host fixture explicitly accepts overflow before complete table occupancy but requires `attributed_calls + overflow_calls == total_calls` and matching nanoseconds, and retains exact 35-key multithread attribution at normal occupancy. Bounded runtime overhead takes priority over per-command detail when saturated. No test run.

A failed `sceKernelMapDirectMemory` returning a **different output pointer** was previously treated like an ordinary failure: the caller released physical backing despite uncertain VA alias ownership. Now each of these paths **aborts on an error-mutated output** rather than risk releasing a still-mapped physical page. Ordinary errors with unchanged pointer still return gracefully, and successful but out-of-window aliases still unmap before direct-memory release. Source contracts staged; no hardware incident attributed to this case and no runtime test performed.

All prior `EDEN_DEV_HLE` line format remains, so frame analysis can inspect hot services; `EDEN_DEV_HLE_OVERFLOW` continues to distinguish capacity loss. Worst-case per-frame-report printf calls from this section are bounded (32+2) instead of 4096+.
- Source preflight asserts bounded reporting. No gameplay/build/benchmark or profiler impact measurement performed.

### 2026-10-09 — Fast negative ownership filter for direct large malloc/free [skip ci]

- Audited the heap's per-call ownership dispatch: when **any** >=32 MiB direct-owned allocation was alive, `eden_heap_large_find()` previously scanned up to **256 atomic address slots** for every foreign `free`, `realloc`, or `malloc_usable_size`, even though almost all libc pointers have never belonged to an owned direct block. This is avoidable guest CPU cache/memory contention.
- Added a **4 KiB monotonic two-hash Bloom filter** in `headless/heap_arenas.inc`, populated **before** publishing each successfully allocated large block. The lookup skips all 256 slots when either membership bit is absent; positive pointers still use the exact original address comparison, and past bits never clear (so stale positives may cost work but may NEVER wrongly route an owned block to libc). No new dynamic allocation, mutex, physical backing or change to direct ownership/free semantics.
- Host growth fixture now keeps a real large block live while it exercises foreign libc allocation → wrap realloc → wrap free 1000 times, asserting the owned block remains Bloom-positive and at least one foreign pointer is filtered. **Not run**, not native-qualified, no claimed FC27 FPS boost.

### 2026-10-09 — Isolate heavy development PC snapshot from guest CPU workers [skip ci]

- `Performance::Snapshot()` runs every five seconds from the PS5 render thread and from optional host captures. The snapshot's syscalls, print loops and block dumps use those local copies after unlocking. Added `EDEN_DEV_SNAPSHOT_COST mono_ns=… elapsed_ns=…` in development to expose the report's own wall-time disturbance. The GPU render thread can still stall running the report, but guest worker publication and PC polling do not wait for its heavy formatting.
- Source static guards added; no host tests, SDK native build or console FPS measurements run.

### 2026-10-09 — Rolling PC profiler samples and bounded guest sampler thread lifetime [skip ci]

- In development `pc_fast=on`, a detached 500 Hz guest-core signal thread previously ran until the fixed **65,536-sample** array filled, then profiling silently stopped (~131 s after fast sampling began); the native GPU sample buffer similarly stopped at 8192 samples (~16 s at 500 Hz). That breaks FC27 profiling later in the same match or after an in-process relaunch, and a detached sampler can outlive the guest thread.
- For normal PC-only mode, sample slots now use **lock-free atomic uintptr_t circular buffers** (8192 GPU, 65536 guest). The single-owner signal handler stores its PC before release-publishing the monotonically increasing sequence number; Snapshot reads with acquire, clamps to the last capacity entries, and explicitly logs `EDEN_PERF_PC_SAMPLES_LOST source=gpu|guest count=N` when consumer lag drops older PCs. Concurrent wrap accesses use atomic slots. Special `EDEN_DEV_WAIT_CALLERS` remains GPU-bounded because its eight-word caller chains are not atomic.
- Replaced the detached 500 Hz generator with a **thread-local `std::jthread`** on the owner guest CPU thread. Its 45-second boot delay is chunked into 200ms stop-aware sleeps; teardown requests stop and joins instead of leaking sampler workers across games. Guest sampler TLS registration clears `core_sample_ready` on exit. The sampler is placed on verified secondary slots where available. Source guards staged; no native compile, signal ABI validation, or firmware timing test yet.

### 2026-10-09 — Clear stale GPU/CPU thread registrations at owner exit [skip ci]

- An in-process return to Library followed by another title destroys/recreates native GPU and guest CPU workers. Previous `RegisterWorker()` wrote `workers[i].registered=true` but never cleared it on thread exit; `PollGpuPc()` could target an exited/recycled GPU `pthread_t`, and periodic worker snapshots could report stale CPU clocks/affinity as current.
- Added a tiny `thread_local WorkerRegistration` owner hook that locks `workers_mutex` only **on thread exit**, verifies the recorded `pthread_t` still matches the slot and marks the slot unregistered. TLS hook is touched before the PC sampler objects so C++ reverse destruction joins the fast `std::jthread` first, then clears core readiness, then unregisters the worker. No new GPU/guest hotpath lock and no false claim that a kill begun before teardown can be canceled retroactively.
- Existing `PollGpuPc()` now copies the registered GPU target under the mutex and sends only after unlock. Source contract checks identity, registration and construction order. No native execution, thread lifecycle test or GitHub Actions triggered.

Scenario A exits and clears its registered GPU slot; scenario B registers a **new** GPU thread over the slot while old owner A is still alive, and A's destructor must **not** clear the newer owner. B then exits and clears itself. Added to the next explicit GitHub Actions source preflight. This is NOT a PS5 native signal/kernel lifetime qualification; no CI or executable run initiated.

### 2026-10-09 — Unify PS5 virtual reservation VA ownership for heap, sparse tables and JIT [skip ci]

Wired all five reservation families to the same helper. Existing caller rollback still owns already successful RW reservations when RX fails, and sparse-table partial alias failure now checks `munmap` return. This is a hardening of exceptional paths, not proof the firmware uses this ABI or the memory OOM is fixed.

- 2026-10-10 potential legitimate pointer-delta-zero recovery: pinned Eden `PageEntryData::Pointer()` treats zero as "missing"; MapPages stores host-minus-guest page base, so coincident host and guest VAs produce a ZERO delta across a contiguous mapping. In source, `GetPointerImpl` incorrectly rejects this, preventing GPU DeviceMemoryManager Map from seeing a valid backing page and producing missing-texture/unmapped-read symptoms. New pinned `eden-ps5-guest-zero-alias.patch` detects it ONLY if entire read/write address lies within real DeviceMemory backing (base + KSystemControl::Init::GetIntendedMemorySize), restores direct host pointer in CPU GetPointerImpl and WalkBlock, and records guest_alias_mapped/guest_alias_access counts. All other invalid pages still fail closed. Strong source-level candidate for FC27 black backgrounds/5120 map asserts, not verified on hardware. No build/CI/native tests run.

- 2026-10-10 guest CPU direct-span correctness source-only: pinned Eden `Memory::Impl::GetSpan` checks `(addr+size)>>12` even though the final touched byte is `addr+size-1`, so page-aligned spans check a spurious next page (and can index outside max AS). Same initial/final block number does not guarantee intermediate pages are still mapped. Added `eden-ps5-guest-span.patch`: PS5-only range bounds, correct final page, O(1) single-page fast path, validate intervening page pointer deltas/types/blocks for multi-page spans, const/nonconst share one algorithm. This may prevent malformed texture/CPU buffer direct access; uncompiled and untested on PS5. Source test and backport validator included.

- 2026-10-10 sparse table physical-slot commit vs decommit interleaving: `ZeroRegion` clears host-page bitmap entries and decommits a 2 MiB private slot when the bitmap looks empty. `CommitPage` previously raced the final scan so a new writer could publish a page then have the entire slot remapped read-only/shared zero (lost data and potential fault). Native generated sparse header now uses **per SparseLargeVector** `std::mutex` held by `ZeroRegion` across bitmap + decommit and by `CommitPage` across CommitSparsePage + bitmap publication; double-check `IsCommittedPage` after locking. `GetOrDefault` and already committed accesses retain lock-free fast paths. The test `tools/check-sparse-index-boundary.py` checks CMake injected locking alongside prior one-past-end fix. This is source-only, not compiled on PS5; externally concurrent logical remap vs an actual write still requires caller sync.

- 2026-10-10 native SparseRange lifetime hardening: `src/memory_pages.cpp::CommitSparsePage` previously read `SparseRangeOf(page)` and dereferenced `range->owned[slot]` **before** `sparse_mutex`. Concurrent `FreeSparsePages` under the mutex can deallocate that bitmap or reuse the range record -> use-after-free/wrong physical slot mapping. Moved the sparse mutex acquisition to the beginning of `CommitSparsePage` and performed range lookup, bitmap check and `OwnSlot` all under the same lock. Only a first-commit slow path takes this lock; ordinary sparse-table hot reads stay lock-free. Added a strict order check to `tools/check-sparse-index-boundary.py`. Works together with per-vector ZeroRegion/CommitPage synchronization, but cannot legalize accessing a SparseLargeVector object after its destructor. No PS5 build/hardware validation.

- 2026-10-10 PS5 sparse table stale-page contents: generated `SparseLargeVector::DecommitPage` previously cleared the HostPageSize commit bitmap and, when any OTHER page in the same 2 MiB physical slot remained committed, simply returned without zeroing the retired host page. Re-committing that host page called `CommitSparsePage`, which found slot already owned and did not remap/zero; old guest/GPU page translations could silently resurrect. Dense-fallback ZeroRegion was broken similarly because DecommitSparsePage is a no-op for dense allocation. Fixed in derived `sparse_private_replacement`: memset the retiring known-committed host page to zero *before* clearing its bitmap/deciding whether to return or reclaim entire slot. Uses existing per-vector ZeroRegion/CommitPage mutation lock, no extra per-frame work. New source order/model regression in `tools/check-sparse-index-boundary.py`. No host compiler or PS5 binary test yet.

- 2026-10-10 CPU→GPU reverse-map inline callback hardening: pinned `src/core/device_memory_manager.h::ApplyOpOnPAddr` read `compressed_device_addr[physical_page]` without validating `physical_page`, and treated reverse value 0 (no GPU mapping) as GPU VA 0, causing unrelated invalidation. `ApplyOpOnPointer` subtracted `physical_base` without checking p was inside actual backing, and `GetPhysicalRawAddressFromDAddr` indexed the GPU table without checking virtual page range. Added pinned header backport `eden-ps5-gpu-reverse-inline-bounds.patch` (three hunks), zero-mapping fast return, DRAM and GPU VA bounds. No global mapping lock added on per-texture hot reads. Header implementation works on both host/PS5 for consistent inline definitions across translation units. Source validator wired; no host compiler/native PS5 FPS validation.

- 2026-10-10 further GPU multi-reverse safety: pinned `MultiAddressContainer::Unregister` can return zero new head when it removed a last remaining node from a corrupted/degenerate "multi" reverse list, but Map and Unmap previously called `ReleaseEntry(0)` and dereferenced `storage[-1]`. Added `eden-ps5-gpu-empty-multi-head.patch` as pinned post-remap source backport to return reverse sentinel zero on empty head, leaving ordinary two-node transitions unchanged. Applied in both remap and unmap, source validator guards both. This is fail-closed defense for malformed reverse state, not a claim of renderer/FPS repair; no PS5 compile/test.

- **2026-10-10 R250 — CPU 16 hardware vs 13 affinity vs eight secondary threads clarified; shader-pool underutilization fix:** User correctly asked whether prior "8 threads × 60,000 operations" implied PS5 Encore only uses 8 CPUs. **No:** it was solely an ASan/TSan contention fixture. The R237 *probe-thread* telemetry reported `hardware_reported=16` and `affinity_allowed=13` with mask `0x1fff`, topology `5` prioritized logical CPU slots (four Switch guest cores and GPU) and `8` isolated secondary logical slots (`0x1fe0`). These are distinct workload/affinity categories, **not** 8 maximum PS5 threads or proof that all 16 SMT contexts are available to this process/firmware. `9ad8e724` fixes another real shader worker throttle in `tools/prepare-vulkan-port.py`: when the OS returns an actual non-empty affinity mask, `schedulable` is its cardinality, **not** `min(affinity_allowed, std::thread::hardware_concurrency())`; `hardware_concurrency()` is only a hint and could underreport granted CPUs, falsely producing one builder. - **2026-10-10 R254 — avoid unsigned overflow in Vulkan texture residency/GC budgets:** The pinned Eden `RunGarbageCollector` PS5 Vulkan prefetch used `u64 usage = total_used_memory; usage -= ReclaimedBytes(image);` with no underflow protection. PS5 RADV/Vulkan may sample total driver allocations while `ReclaimedBytes` sums logical texture cost, so deferred frees or alias counts can exceed projected remaining usage; u64 wrap yields enormous bogus pressure, over-prefetching GPU readbacks/`runtime.Finish` calls rather than stopping. `0690a951` adds nonallocating constexpr `headless/vulkan_gc_budget.h` with saturating `AfterProjectedEviction(used,reclaimed)`; `177567dc` uses it on the dirty-texture prefetch lookahead; `b538bef5` wires its include into the real generated `texture_cache.h`. `d17dfb1f`/`c93ae503` apply the same overflow protection to both actual `DeleteImage` texture-cost decrements for Vulkan only, while leaving OpenGL's existing accounting untouched; corrected generated C++ escaped-newline handling before host CI. `044a1f80`/`9df74881` add a C++20 ASan+UBSan 200,000-case header regression, literal AST extraction of both actual Vulkan DeleteImage source transformations, and original OpenGL preservation assertions. `a4f6ed83`/`02b16cdb` add it to existing host-only and future PS5 build preflights; **NO native PS5 build**. This prevents **one** possible repeated GC stall/false-pressure path, **not** proof that FC27 black textures/low FPS disappear, or of maximum PS5 CPU/GPU/RAM/GDDR6 hardware utilization. Old host test `#38045197373` was fully green before these R254 code changes; rerun host-only CI on R254 source and record actual outcome.

- **2026-10-10 R257 / PS5 Vulkan dev pipeline trace thread safety:** Found a separate C++ UB/crash risk in the Vulkan generator `shader_costs` code: its optional developer `trace_pipelines=on` first-use logging inserted pipeline pointers from concurrently building graphics/compute shader workers into mutable function-static `std::unordered_set<const void*>` **without synchronization**, then read `traced.size()` during formatting. Besides the data race, this static set lived for the entire launcher process and could suppress traces when VkPipeline pointers were recycled by a new title, while accumulating unbounded allocations during many games. `a32b1846` adds `headless/pipeline_trace_registry.h`, a per-title epoch-aware `FirstUseRegistry` guarded by `std::mutex`, with fixed max 65,536 pipeline IDs per type, one graphics and one compute instance, using pre-existing `gpu_time_session` atomic title generation. `9d1702b7` refactors both generator sites to obtain insertion ordinal under the mutex but perform snprintf/kernel-log **after the lock**, with no work when tracing is off. `fa953685` adds C++20 ASan/UBSan and Linux TSan 16-worker test for unique first-use ordinals, duplicate suppression, title epoch reset, reused pointer attribution, separate shader classes and bound; `a94b3ab6`/`b1bdce15` wire it to existing host/native source preflights. **Source/host test not yet run on these commits**, so do not claim a green gate or a PS5 native fix. - **2026-10-10 R258 / Vulkan GC staging ownership correction (host gate requested):** The prefetch path in `headless/vulkan_gc_downloads.inc` stored staging map descriptors by **copy**, before calling `image.DownloadMemory(map, copies)`, unlike the already validated normal Vulkan readback path that moves its descriptor into the deferred batch. This can duplicate ownership of RAII handles or break with a move-only map, and may let a temporary mapping disappear before `runtime.Finish()`. `ff683984` records the GPU copy on the still-owned local map and then uses `gc_downloads.emplace_back(id, std::move(map))`, leaving a single deferred owner until `Finish`/publication/release. New `tools/check-vulkan-gc-staging-lifetime-host.py` extracts and compiles those **literal** production statements against non-copyable C++20 maps under ASan+UBSan, checking 1 and 16 in-flight transfers and no destruction before the fence. Both host-only and native *prebuild* workflows call this fixture. This note marks a **[core-ready] host-only preflight request**, not a tested success or a PS5-native compilation. No proof yet of live driver transfer integrity, FC27/BOtW FPS, missing-texture elimination, 13.60 SDK compatibility, physical heap reclamation or full CPU/GPU/RAM/GDDR6 utilization. Keep issue #8 open; production release untouched.

- **2026-10-10 R259 — pinned Vulkan staging audit corrects R258 premise, and fixes actual early-frame GC overflow:** Inspection of the EXACT upstream Eden mirror at pinned commit `5f142c7926d0c7fcbbd0ce30794d72f638a43b2a` shows `vk_staging_buffer_pool.h::StagingBufferRef` is a trivially copyable, non-owning value descriptor (VkBuffer, span, offset, usage, level, index); the pool, **not the descriptor destructor**, owns its lifetime. Hence R258's claim that copying this handle caused a RAII double-release/UAF was **incorrect**, and its move-only host mock was not representative of production. Restored the original staging reference copy-before-recording ordering and removed the misleading mock from both CI gates. R258's prior host green result proves only that synthetic model, NOT a real Vulkan lifecycle fix. Independently verified `texture_cache_base.h` initializes unsigned `u64 frame_tick = 0`, while the pinned `RunGarbageCollector` passes `frame_tick - ticks_to_destroy` to `lru_cache.ForEachItemBelow` **twice**, with ages 10/25/50. When immediate texture pressure enters GC during the first frames, unsigned subtraction wraps. The pinned LRU's signed comparison actually treats the wrapped number as a negative sentinel and normally **skips**, rather than evicts, new textures; the earlier claim that this alone explained startup texture loss was incorrect. We make that grace period explicit so it cannot regress when removing the wrap. Added nonallocating `::Eden::VulkanMemory::OldestEvictionTick(frame_tick, min_age)` to `headless/vulkan_gc_budget.h` (clamps to 0). The pinned generator now fail-closed checks/replaces **all three** LRU cutoff expressions (one Vulkan lookahead and two common cleanup loops), and guards the lookahead and BOTH cleanup passes so an inclusive LRU cutoff of zero cannot accidentally evict textures touched at frame zero. The policy after age threshold is unchanged. Extended the existing `tools/check-vulkan-gc-budget-host.py` with source transformation/age-guard checks and C++20 ASan/UBSan tests for 0..109 frames, all three GC age thresholds, boundary/UINT64_MAX and 200,000 randomized cases. **HOST CI NOT YET DISPATCHED** as of this note; request only `[core-ready]` preflight, no native build. This is a bounded correctness fix, not observed PS5 FPS/texture proof or CPU/GPU/GDDR6 saturation. Issue #8 remains open.

- **R259 follow-up correction / host-only validation request:** The pinned `common/lru_cache.h::ForEachItemBelow` includes its cutoff; replacing wrapped u64 with zero without an age gate would turn safe no-eviction at frame 0 into an early eviction. Added `frame_tick >= ticks_to_destroy` guards for both generated GC cleanup loops and `frame_tick < ticks_to_destroy` early return inside Vulkan prefetch. The new `OldestEvictionTick` helper only runs after the age guard. Host tests now assert the 3 cutoff substitutions, 2 cleanup guards and early Vulkan prefetch exit. The R259 source diff remains **unverified until this [core-ready] run completes**; no PS5-native build or hardware performance claim.

Added the standard C++ header. This marker requests a new host-only [core-ready] preflight; previous green R258 and prior red R259 do not qualify the new source. Native PS5 build remains skipped, production untouched.

- **2026-10-10 R261 — mixed clean/dirty Vulkan GC LRU lookahead now skips, not aborts, non-prefetchable entries (HOST CI REQUESTED):** Audited real pinned Eden `5f142c7926d0c7fcbbd0ce30794d72f638a43b2a` `TextureCache<P>::RunGarbageCollector` and `common/lru_cache.h::ForEachItemBelow`: the real Cleanup loop consumes an iteration and continues past clean, aliased, scaled and multisample images, but the PS5 Vulkan `PrefetchDownloads` formerly returned TRUE at the first such image. - **2026-10-10 R262 — skip unbatchable oversized Vulkan GC image, keep subsequent small dirty batch eligible (HOST CI REQUESTED):** R261 let the 40-entry LRU prefetch skip clean/aliased/scaled/multisample entries. This does NOT increase real GPU memory budget, does not remove large-image's mandatory GPU wait and has no PS5 hardware FPS proof. - **2026-10-10 R270 -- physical large-block accounting correction, source-only, UNVERIFIED:** R269 formerly summed requested large-heap allocation sizes as direct physical usage. A separate eden_heap_large_physical_bytes atomic counts the real backing for each dedicated large block. EDEN_DIRECT_POOL_OWNERS now uses this counter while preserving requested heap_large_bytes and explicitly logging coverage when the optional owner hook is unavailable. Linux allocator mock maps the full Sony-modeled span, and extended heap/accounting source gates assert physical counters on large allocate, free, and realloc-to-zero. **No host CI run, no PS5 native build, no PS5 FPS or actual reclamation proof.** Root mspaces (historical 1280 MiB) still stay allocated; console PS5 exposes 12 GiB direct extent, not guaranteed free 16 GiB. - **2026-10-10 R271 — reset PS5 GPU direct-memory pressure snapshots on every new title (SOURCE ONLY, HOST CI PENDING):** Native `headless/performance.cpp::RefreshFreeMemory()` previously retained process-global `checked_ns`, `largest_free_block`, `graphics_memory_short`, `has_valid_sample` and consecutive query failures across game sessions. In the same launcher process a new title may start with a different GPU and guest direct-memory footprint, so the old title's observed largest-free block is not authoritative even for the next 100-ms interval; it may spur needless dirty-texture evictions or delay pressure awareness. Moved these atomics to namespace storage, added `ResetDirectMemoryProbeForTitle()` called from `headless/main.cpp` immediately after the existing per-title frame timestamp reset (after previous renderer worker teardown). The reset refuses an unexpected in-flight query, clears all old known-sample state/timestamp and forces conservative low-memory status until the first fresh kernel sample; no per-frame allocations, additional kernel queries, cache budget changes, unsafe physical root reclamation or native PS5 build. Source-only contracts added in performance and R269 account host checks. CI still required. **Does not prove FC27 stutters/textures fixed or recover old 1,280 MiB roots.**

- **2026-10-10 R272 — bounded PS5 GPU-thread direct-memory probe cadence (HOST CI REQUESTED):** `RefreshFreeMemory()` previously synchronously called `sceKernelAvailableDirectMemorySize` as often as once per 100 ms on the GPU-thread cache path, even when the kernel had confirmed large free direct-memory headroom. Added pure C++20 `headless/direct_pool_probe_policy.h` and host sanitizer assertions: keep baseline **100 ms** for unknown/failed/low-memory samples; only if the *previous successful kernel observation* had **>=4 GiB largest contiguous free block**, reduce sampling to **250 ms** until a new measurement reduces headroom or an error occurs. The added three relaxed/acquire atomic reads execute only AFTER the original 100-ms fast-return (not on normal 60/120 FPS frames), and single-flight kernel query ownership remains unchanged. The first query after the R271 per-title reset is immediate. Does not raise heap/JIT/Vulkan budgets or free any Sony direct pages. This lowers the maximum **query frequency** from 10/s to 4/s in healthy high-headroom conditions only; PS5 latency/FPS gains remain **UNPROVEN**. - **2026-10-10 R274 — conservative PS5 direct-memory region enumeration provenance (HOST CI REQUESTED):** `ReportDirectMemoryState` already visited up to 8192 kernel direct allocation records once at lifecycle stages and computed `total - summed_intervals` as an UPPER bound on direct free memory. It previously logged `scan_valid=1` even when `sceKernelDirectMemoryQuery` stopped early, misleading consumers into treating a structurally valid partial scan as complete. New `headless/direct_pool_region_scan.h` validates monotonically ordered, nonoverlapping, in-extent physical intervals with overflow-safe occupied lower-bound arithmetic; PS5 lifecycle logs preserve legacy `scan_valid` meaning (record validity) and add `scan_reached_extent`, `scan_stop` (0 physical extent reached; 1 kernel returned nonzero; 2 invalid record; 3 bounded 8192-record limit) and `query_rc`, and explicitly retain `free_upper` as an upper bound **never admission/free budget**. C++20 ASan/UBSan host tests cover interrupted, overlapping, out-of-range and end-reached scenarios. No renderer/GPU hot-path changes, no Sony physical unmap, no native PS5 SDK build; R273 root retention and 1,280-MiB retained memory remain open. Host [core-ready] gate requested.

- **2026-10-10 R275 — reject impossible kernel direct-memory largest-free reports before JIT/GPU admission (HOST CI REQUESTED):** Following R274's distinction between partial region enumeration and free-memory admissions, audited **all three** `sceKernelAvailableDirectMemorySize` consumers. Previously an rc=0 reply was trusted even if returned `start` was negative / beyond the 12-GiB PS5 direct extent or `largest` extended beyond the physical end; this could poison admission budgets and diagnostics. New pure `Eden::DirectPool::IsKernelFreeSpanValid(extent,start,length)` checks signed extent, nonnegative start and end-span using subtraction (no addition overflow). `QueryLargestDirectMemoryBlock` returns false with zero output for invalid range (no expanded dense JIT). GPU `RefreshFreeMemory` treats invalid success as a missed query, retaining its conservative pressure policy. `ReportDirectMemoryState` distinguishes syscall success from validated free span: `largest_valid=0` and `largest_free_known=0` when impossible. C++20 ASan/UBSan compile-time boundary cases cover 0, exact limit, negative/overflow, and max-size values. In R274 host contract, replaced a tautological assertion with a real source invariant. No extra kernel probes, CPU/GPU frame work, mspace unmap, changed allocation budget, or PS5 SDK build. Host [core-ready] requested, native evidence & 1,280-MiB physical reclamation remain OPEN.

- **2026-10-10 R276 — eliminate default multi-thousand-syscall direct region walks on title lifecycle (HOST CI REQUESTED):** `ReportDirectMemoryState` ran up to **8192 synchronous `sceKernelDirectMemoryQuery` calls per lifecycle report** (core_initialized, core_shutdown, core_destroyed), including normal PS5 app sessions. The region enumeration is informational only: neither JIT admission, renderer pressure probe nor per-owner physical ledger uses its free upper bound. New default-off `direct_region_scan_enabled` is updated once per game from an explicit `memory-region-scan.txt` marker under the existing Eden app file root, and gates the entire expensive kernel scan. With no marker, diagnostic emits `scan_stop=4`, `scan_valid=0`, `scan_reached_extent=0`, `free_upper=-1`; separately reports kernel-confirmed largest-free and the R269/R270 owner ledger without suppression. With marker present, retains the exact R274 bounded 8192-region scan and stop provenance. `tools/check-ps5-direct-pool-host.py` and `tools/check-runtime-frame-budget.py` lock the source contract. This saves up to 3 * 8192 kernel-query calls per normal game lifecycle IF the scan previously hit its maximum; actual PS5 timing/FPS effect is unmeasured. No physical mspace unmap, no broader RAM budget, no native PS5 build; source-only host gate requested.

- **2026-10-10 R277 — recover Vulkan pool capacity from a transient failed first direct-memory probe (HOST CI REQUESTED):** The RADV/Vulkan `Device` patch from `tools/prepare-vulkan-port.py` stored `graphics_capacity=free_memory()` exactly once during `VkDevice` init. If Sony's first query returned zero/unknown (R271 fail-closed path), capacity stayed ZERO even after later valid kernel observations. That permanently disabled the low-pool scaling used to align the 6.4-GiB Vulkan cache budget with true initial contiguous PS5 pool capacity, potentially mis-triggering dirty-texture GC. Introduces `headless/vulkan_pool_capacity.h::PoolCapacityLatch`, an allocation-free atomic per-process latch **reset for every new VkDevice**, with an initial nonzero observation or the first later valid nonzero sample. `GetDeviceMemoryUsage` obtains **one** current observation from the existing throttled callback and passes it to the latch (no doubled kernel calls, no new frame-region scan, no changed budget). `tools/check-vulkan-gc-budget-host.py` compiles the actual latch and exercises zero-start, subsequent recovery, reset and eight-thread contention under ASan/UBSan; pinned generated Vulkan source contracts asserted. Does not use unclassified kernel memory as free, does not expose Sony system RAM, does not physically unmap 1,280-MiB heap roots. Source only, not a measured FC27 frame-time fix. Native build skipped; `[core-ready]` requested.

- **2026-10-10 R278 — short-circuit Vulkan dirty texture prefetch when forbidden by memory policy (HOST CI REQUESTED):** In the pinned Vulkan GPU collector, `PrefetchDownloads` always walked up to `num_iterations` LRU images even when `DirtyEvictions()` was false, spending GPU-thread time traversing clean/dirty entries and re-evaluating `KeepDirtyTextures()`/throttled Sony direct-pool pressure for each dirty image, despite never staging dirty downloads. R278 checks `const bool prefetch_dirty = DirtyEvictions(); if (!prefetch_dirty) return;` immediately after releasing any previous deferred staging handles, before any LRU walk. The per-image repeated memory policy probe is removed from this **prefetch-only** walk: one stable policy decision per pass. Unchanged actual Cleanup still deletes eligible clean textures, preserves dirty images when protected, and continues to use its own normal pressure evaluation; when dirty download is allowed, all existing 40-descriptor/32-MiB batch limits, staged transfers, `runtime.Finish`, and fallback paths remain identical. Host regression contracts cover bypass ordering and original mixed-image behavior; no native Sony GPU test and no numerical FPS claims. Source remains feature-branch only; `[core-ready]` requested.

- **2026-10-10 R279 — avoid early-frame PS5 GPU memory queries; one fixed projected-eviction threshold per Vulkan prefetch (HOST CI REQUESTED):** Follow-up to R278: its short-circuit initially ran `DirtyEvictions()` before the pinned LRU age gate, unnecessarily consulting the Sony direct-memory pressure callback at the beginning of each game even though no textures can qualify for eviction. R279 moves `if (frame_tick < ticks_to_destroy) return;` directly after deferred-stage cleanup and **before** `DirtyEvictions()`. When textures can qualify, it computes `projected_stop` once per speculative batch from the pressure policy, not for each of up to 40 clean/dirty LRU images. Actual `Cleanup` keeps its own current-pressure decision, age/grace handling, capacity, GPU-fence and fallback correctness; only speculative prefetch CPU work changes. Updated exact source extraction in `check-vulkan-gc-mixed-prefetch-host.py`, with aging/ordering/one-probe assertions in multiple host gates. No claimed FC27 gain without native PS5 timing; no memory backing freed; development branch only, `[core-ready]` requested.

Dirty texture prefetch LRU walk now bypassed when protection policy forbids dirty evictions; actual Cleanup still handles clean textures and uses authoritative pressure policy. - **R279 VERIFIED host-only:** [Encore core CI #38066778701](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38066778701) on source SHA `3b9ab29f437be4eece7933478cb8d84ab094b732` **SUCCESS** all host stages including source-extracted Vulkan GC projection and ASan/UBSan mixed-state fixtures; native PS5 #38066778645 **SKIPPED**. - **2026-10-10 R280 — Vulkan present worker in-flight completion / error propagation (HOST CI REQUESTED):** Compared exact pinned Eden mirror `5f142c79` present manager with current PS5 generated patch. `WaitPresent()` could return when present queue became empty merely because worker popped its final frame, even though `CopyToSwapchain()` was still in progress, and earlier `present_failure || queue.empty()` patch could silently return after exception. Source generator now adds `present_in_flight` under queue mutex (set on pop, clear only on successful swapchain copy + free frame handoff; catch clears/publishes failure under queue+free locks), waits for `(empty && !in_flight) || failure`, and rethrows on failure. Worker releases swapchain mutex before acquiring queue mutex on success, avoiding reverse lock-order nesting. Original swapchain drain lock, frame GPU fences and image barriers remain. New host sanitizer concurrency stand-in and production generator anchor assertions run via core CI. This addresses a proven state-machine lifecycle race, **not** validated FC27 frame timing or missing textures. No native PS5 build, no RAM unmap, shipping untouched.

- **2026-10-10 R281 — release free-frame mutex before blocking on a Vulkan present fence (HOST CI REQUESTED):** In the exact pinned `vk_present_manager.cpp`, `PresentManager::GetRenderFrame()` held `free_mutex` during `frame->present_done.Wait()` and `.Reset()` even after popping that frame from the queue. Since `PresentThread()` needs the same mutex to return *other* completed frames into `free_queue`, a long GPU fence wait could serially block the producer's completion handoff and amplify frame backlog. Generated PS5 patch now explicitly `lock.unlock()` immediately after the successful `free_queue.pop_front()` ownership transfer, before the fence wait, preserving the original GPU fence Wait/Reset order, ownership and synchronization; with R280 `present_in_flight`, wait-drain accounting remains intact. New host concurrent stand-in creates a blocked per-frame fence and uses `try_lock` to assert the unrelated frame producer can acquire the free-queue mutex/recycle another frame during it; covered by existing R280 host ASan/UBSan/TSan test and the `[core-ready]` suite. Does NOT eliminate a real GPU fence wait, change image barriers, claim improved PS5 FPS or free 1,280 MiB. Console firmware timing remains UNQUALIFIED; native build skipped.

- **R280 VERIFIED host-only:** [core source CI #38067528391](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38067528391) SHA `b474c701ca7df4b62515f0269dc1380f75cf2754` **SUCCESS full host suite**. The pinned Vulkan present manager patch now waits for both empty queued frames AND no in-flight popped frame; worker exceptions wake waiters and are propagated, rather than allowing silent 'present complete'. Host ASan/UBSan/TSan concurrent stand-in verifies frame success and exception-after-pop; code generator anchors retained. Native PS5 run #38067528362 **SKIPPED**. The pinned native Vulkan/PS5 SDK binary is not built and frame-time benefit is not yet measured.
- **R281 VERIFIED host-only:** [core source CI #38067761638](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38067761638) SHA `436a11b88f29c25df23a485d9b31fb79966fb661` **SUCCESS full host suite**, including the new blocked GPU-fence/free-queue try_lock scenario under ASan/UBSan/TSan. `GetRenderFrame` relinquishes free_mutex after popping its exclusively owned frame and before blocking in `frame->present_done.Wait()`, allowing the async present thread to recycle *other* frames; PS5 Vulkan fence Wait/Reset stays authoritative. Native PS5 run #38067761620 **SKIPPED**. FC27 FPS, frozen textures, memory/CPU saturation and real firmware safety are unproven.

This is a real synchronization correctness/liveness improvement but NOT yet a console-tested stutter cure.

- **2026-10-10 R285 — fix PS5-native C++17 GPU atomic publication syntax failure (HOST CI REQUESTED):** First native compile + early syntax gate reached in manual All-On [run #38071649101](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38071649101), SHA `2e901db4`, after ALL host preflight and `make prepare` GPU backport success. Native Clang rejected `std::atomic_ref` in `src/core/device_memory_manager.h:200–209`, visible in generated `headless/sw_blitter_sized.cpp` and dependent translation units: SDK targets use **`-std=c++17`**, while `std::atomic_ref` requires C++20. R285 changes ONLY the production patch forward and reverse inline `AtomicLoad/Store{Physical,Continuity,Backing,Reverse}` helpers to `__atomic_load_n` / `__atomic_store_n` with **the same acquire/release/relaxed memory ordering** and unchanged 16-byte trivially-copyable SparseLargeVector `TrackedEntry` and `u32` reverse slots. PS5 and host GCC/Clang both support GNU atomic builtins in C++17; no mutex or unprotected access introduced. Critical patch replay detail: reversed patch context for previous `AtomicStoreBacking` must match modified forward patch. `tools/apply-eden-backports.sh` validators updated to exact builtins; pinned upstream 16-patch host replay asserts 4 typed loads + 4 stores and absence of C++20 `std::atomic_ref`; exact extracted production 8-helper C++ host concurrency test moved to `-std=c++17` under ASan/UBSan + TSan with 8/16-thread simultaneous remaps. This is a **real native compiler incompatibility fix**, not claimed FC27 performance improvement; build stopped before binary, no package or console test. Shipping untouched. Native dev All-On needs a new run on corrected SHA only after green source CI.

- **2026-10-10 R286 — fix stale PS5 GPU atomic source assertion after R285 native C++17 repair (HOST CI REQUESTED):** Initial R285 host core [#38072332207](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38072332207) stopped at `check-gpu-memory-mapping-source.py:104`, which still demanded the removed `std::atomic_ref`; no production failure proven. R286 updates the six forward and two reverse atomic source tokens to the exact `__atomic_load_n` / `__atomic_store_n` builtin calls and original acquire/release/relaxed order, asserts no C++20 `std::atomic_ref` patch tokens and corrects reverse-context store count including the earlier forward helper. Existing C++17 ASan/UBSan/TSan concurrency and 16-patch pinned upstream source replay tests remain mandatory. The previous native `#38071649101` demonstrated real C++17 incompatibility and produced no PS5 app; R285/R286 require host PASS and then a NEW native dev-branch workflow dispatch.

- **2026-10-10 R287 — cached Eden revalidation after C++17 atomics (HOST CI REQUESTED):** New manually launched native All-On [run #38073074961](https://github.com/niakw/Prospero.Eden-Encore/actions/runs/38073074961), job #114274324627, SHA `e753fb7b50bc18f5cfb65d3b83109c86f22f4fbe`, completed **FAILURE** at `Prepare native build inputs → make prepare → validate_ps5_gpu_memory_mapping`, **BEFORE** native compilation. Exact log `Pinned PS5 GPU memory backport missing: invalid.continuity_tracker = 0;`. Root cause: `apply_one()` revalidates old cached patch receipt against **the final fully-upgraded source**; R285 replaced plain continuity field stores with C++17 `AtomicStoreContinuity` and physical load with `AtomicLoadPhysical`. Initial validator still demanded older data-racy direct expressions even when atomic patch receipt has been applied. R287 treats initial mapped stage (all four legacy expressions) OR final atomic stage (all four atomic expressions) as valid; atomic stage additionally requires correct `__atomic` helper definitions and no legacy data-racy raw page references. It does not remove synchronization or reset installed/OS memory.
